=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a positive integer N. Determine whether there exists a pair of positive integers (x,y) such that x^3 - y^3 = N. If such a pair exists, print one such pair (x,y).

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N

Output

If there is no pair of positive integers (x,y) satisfying x^3 - y^3 = N, print -1.
If there is such a pair, print x and y in this order separated by a space. If there are multiple solutions, printing any one of them is accepted as correct.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 10^{18}
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

397

Sample Output 1

12 11

We have 12^3 - 11^3 = 397, so (x,y) = (12,11) is a solution.

Sample Input 2

1

Sample Output 2

-1

No pair of positive integers (x,y) satisfies x^3 - y^3 = 1. Thus, print -1.

Sample Input 3

39977273855577088

Sample Output 3

342756 66212

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's implement integer cube root robustly:
def icbrt(n):
    lo, hi = 0, 10**6+1 # since n<=1e18, hi maybe 10**6+1
    while hi - lo > 1:
        mid = (lo+hi)//2
        if mid*mid*mid <= n:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    return lo

Let's maybe implement:
import sys, math

def solve():
    N = int(sys.stdin.readline())
    for d in range(1, 10**6 + 1):
        if d*d*d > N:
            break
        if N % d != 0:
            continue
        m = N // d
        delta = 3 * (4*m - d*d)
        s = math.isqrt(delta)
        if s*s != delta:
            continue
        num = s - 3*d
        if num <= 0 or num % 6 != 0:
            continue
        y = num // 6
        x = y + d
        # optional verify
        if x*x*x - y*y*y == N:
            print(x, y)
            return
    print(-1)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()

Let's think about code style:
```python
import sys
from math import isqrt

def solve():
    N = int(sys.stdin.readline())
    for d in range(1, 10**6 + 1):
        if d * d * d > N:
            break
        if N % d:
            continue
        m = N // d
        delta = 3 * (4 * m - d * d)
        s = isqrt(delta)
        if s * s != delta:
            continue
        num = s - 3 * d
        if num <= 0 or num % 6:
            continue
        y = num // 6
        x = y + d
        if x * x * x - y * y * y == N:
            print(x, y)
            return
    print(-1)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```
This is final.

Now, let's think about if there is a solution with x=82,y=14, N=548624, d=68. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a solution with x=82,y=15, N=547993, d=67. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a solution with x=82
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
.</think>```python
import sys
from math import isqrt

def solve():
    N = int(sys.stdin.readline())

    for d in range(1, 10**6 + 1):
        if d * d * d > N:
            break

        if N % d != 0:
            continue

        m = N // d
        delta = 3 * (4 * m - d * d)
        s = isqrt(delta)

        if s * s != delta:
            continue

        num = s - 3 * d
        if num <= 0 or num % 6 != 0:
            continue

        y = num // 6
        x = y + d

        if x * x * x - y * y * y == N:
            print(x, y)
            return

    print(-1)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```
